else:\n                for part in name.split(LOOKUP_SEP):\n                    klass_infos = (\n                        *klass_info.get(\"related_klass_infos\", []),\n                        *_get_parent_klass_info(klass_info),\n                    )\n                    for related_klass_info in klass_infos:\n                        field = related_klass_info[\"field\"]\n                        if related_klass_info[\"reverse\"]:\n                            field = field.remote_field\n                        if field.name == part:\n                            klass_info = related_klass_info\n                            break\n                    else:\n                        klass_info = None\n                        break\n                if klass_info is None:\n                    invalid_names.append(name)\n                    continue\n                col = _get_first_selected_col_from_model(klass_info)\n            if col is not None:\n                if self.connection.features.select_for_update_of_column:\n                    result.append(self.compile(col)[0])\n                else:\n                    result.append(self.quote_name_unless_alias(col.alias))\n        if invalid_names:\n            raise FieldError(\n                \"Invalid field name(s) given in select_for_update(of=(...)): %s. \"\n                \"Only relational fields followed in the query are allowed. \"\n                \"Choices are: %s.\"\n                % (\n                    \", \".join(invalid_names),\n                    \", \".join(_get_field_choices()),\n                )\n            )\n        return result\n\n    def get_converters(self, expressions):\n        i = 0\n        converters = {}\n\n        for expression in expressions:\n            if isinstance(expression, ColPairs):\n                cols = expression.get_source_expressions()\n                cols_converters = self.get_converters(cols)\n                for j, (convs, col) in cols_converters.items():\n                    converters[i + j] = (convs, col)\n                i += len(expression)\n            elif expression:\n                backend_converters = self.connection.ops.get_db_converters(expression)\n                field_converters = expression.get_db_converters(self.connection)\n                if backend_converters or field_converters:\n                    converters[i] = (backend_converters + field_converters, expression)\n                i += 1\n            else:\n                i += 1\n\n        return converters\n\n    def apply_converters(self, rows, converters):\n        connection = self.connection\n        converters = list(converters.items())\n        for row in map(list, rows):\n            for pos, (convs, expression) in converters:\n                value = row[pos]\n                for converter in convs:\n                    value = converter(value, expression, connection)\n                row[pos] = value\n            yield row\n\n    def has_composite_fields(self, expressions):\n        # Check for composite fields before calling the relatively costly\n        # composite_fields_to_tuples.\n        return any(isinstance(expression, ColPairs) for expression in expressions)\n\n    def composite_fields_to_tuples(self, rows, expressions):\n        col_pair_slices = [\n            slice(i, i + len(expression))\n            for i, expression in enumerate(expressions)\n            if isinstance(expression, ColPairs)\n        ]\n\n        for row in map(list, rows):\n            for pos in col_pair_slices:\n                row[pos] = (tuple(row[pos]),)\n\n            yield row\n\n    def results_iter(\n        self,\n        results=None,\n        tuple_expected=False,\n        chunked_fetch=False,\n        chunk_size=GET_ITERATOR_CHUNK_SIZE,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"Return an iterator over the results from executing this query.\"\"\"\n        if results is None:\n            results = self.execute_sql(\n                MULTI, chunked_fetch=chunked_fetch, chunk_size=chunk_size\n            )\n        fields = [s[0] for s in self.select[0 : self.col_count]]\n        converters = self.get_converters(fields)\n        rows = chain.from_iterable(results)\n        if converters:\n            rows = self.apply_converters(rows, converters)\n        if self.has_composite_fields(fields):\n            rows = self.composite_fields_to_tuples(rows, fields)\n        if tuple_expected:\n            rows = map(tuple, rows)\n        return rows\n\n    def has_results(self):\n        \"\"\"\n        Backends (e.g. NoSQL) can override this in order to use optimized\n        versions of \"query has any results.\"\n        \"\"\"\n        return bool(self.execute_sql(SINGLE))\n\n    def execute_sql(\n        self, result_type=MULTI, chunked_fetch=False, chunk_size=GET_ITERATOR_CHUNK_SIZE\n    ):\n        \"\"\"\n        Run the query against the database and return the result(s). The\n        return value depends on the value of result_type.\n\n        When result_type is:\n        - MULTI: Retrieves all rows using fetchmany(). Wraps in an iterator for\n           chunked reads when supported.\n        - SINGLE: Retrieves a single row using fetchone().\n        - ROW_COUNT: Retrieves the number of rows in the result.\n        - CURSOR: Runs the query, and returns the cursor object. It is the\n           caller's responsibility to close the cursor.\n        \"\"\"\n        result_type = result_type or NO_RESULTS\n        try:\n            sql, params = self.as_sql()\n            if not sql:\n                raise EmptyResultSet\n        except EmptyResultSet:\n            if result_type == MULTI:\n                return iter([])\n            else:\n                return\n        if chunked_fetch:\n            cursor = self.connection.chunked_cursor()\n        else:\n            cursor = self.connection.cursor()\n        try:\n            cursor.execute(sql, params)\n        except Exception:\n            # Might fail for server-side cursors (e.g. connection closed)\n            cursor.close()\n            raise\n\n        if result_type == ROW_COUNT:\n            try:\n                return cursor.rowcount\n            finally:\n                cursor.close()\n        if result_type == CURSOR:\n            # Give the caller the cursor to process and close.\n            return cursor\n        if result_type == SINGLE:\n            try:\n                val = cursor.fetchone()\n                if val:\n                    return val[0 : self.col_count]\n                return val\n            finally:\n                # done with the cursor\n                cursor.close()\n        if result_type == NO_RESULTS:\n            cursor.close()\n            return\n\n        result = cursor_iter(\n            cursor,\n            self.connection.features.empty_fetchmany_value,\n            self.col_count if self.has_extra_select else None,\n            chunk_size,\n        )\n        if not chunked_fetch or not self.connection.features.can_use_chunked_reads:\n            # If we are using non-chunked reads, we return the same data\n            # structure as normally, but ensure it is all read into memory\n            # before going any further. Use chunked_fetch if requested,\n            # unless the database doesn't support it.\n            return list(result)\n        return result\n\n    def explain_query(self):\n        result = list(self.execute_sql())\n        # Some backends return 1 item tuples with strings, and others return\n        # tuples with integers and strings. Flatten them out into strings.\n        format_ = self.query.explain_info.format\n        output_formatter = json.dumps if format_ and format_.lower() == \"json\" else str\n        for row in result:\n            for value in row:\n                if not isinstance(value, str):\n                    yield \" \".join([output_formatter(c) for c in value])\n                else:\n                    yield value\n\n\nclass SQLInsertCompiler(SQLCompiler):\n    returning_fields = None\n    returning_params = ()\n\n    def field_as_sql(self, field, get_placeholder, val):\n        \"\"\"\n        Take a field and a value intended to be saved on that field, and\n        return placeholder SQL and accompanying params. Check for raw values,\n        expressions, and fields with get_placeholder() defined in that order.\n\n        When field is None, consider the value raw and use it as the\n        placeholder, with no corresponding parameters returned.\n        \"\"\"\n        if field is None:\n            # A field value of None means the value is raw.\n            sql, params = val, []\n        elif hasattr(val, \"as_sql\"):\n            # This is an expression, let's compile it.\n            sql, params = self.compile(val)\n        elif get_placeholder is not None:\n            # Some fields (e.g. geo fields) need special munging before\n            # they can be inserted.\n            sql, params = get_placeholder(val, self, self.connection), [val]\n        else:\n            # Return the common case for the placeholder\n            sql, params = \"%s\", [val]\n\n        # The following hook is only used by Oracle Spatial, which sometimes\n        # needs to yield 'NULL' and [] as its placeholder and params instead\n        # of '%s' and [None]. The 'NULL' placeholder is produced earlier by\n        # OracleOperations.get_geom_placeholder(). The following line removes\n        # the corresponding None parameter. See ticket #10888.\n        params = self.connection.ops.modify_insert_params(sql, params)\n\n        return sql, params\n\n    def prepare_value(self, field, value):\n        \"\"\"\n        Prepare a value to be used in a query by resolving it if it is an\n        expression and otherwise calling the field's get_db_prep_save().\n        \"\"\"\n        if hasattr(value, \"resolve_expression\"):\n            value = value.resolve_expression(\n                self.query, allow_joins=False, for_save=True\n            )\n            # Don't allow values containing Col expressions. They refer to\n            # existing columns on a row, but in the case of insert the row\n            # doesn't exist yet.\n            if value.contains_column_references:\n                raise ValueError(\n                    'Failed to insert expression \"%s\" on %s. F() expressions '\n                    \"can only be used to update, not to insert.\" % (value, field)\n                )\n            if value.contains_aggregate:\n                raise FieldError(\n                    \"Aggregate functions are not allowed in this query \"\n                    \"(%s=%r).\" % (field.name, value)\n                )\n            if value.contains_over_clause:\n                raise FieldError(\n                    \"Window expressions are not allowed in this query (%s=%r).\"\n                    % (field.name, value)\n                )\n        return field.get_db_prep_save(value, connection=self.connection)\n\n    def pre_save_val(self, field, obj):\n        \"\"\"\n        Get the given field's value off the given obj. pre_save() is used for\n        things like auto_now on DateTimeField. Skip it if this is a raw query.\n        \"\"\"\n        if self.query.raw:\n            return getattr(obj, field.attname)\n        return field.pre_save(obj, add=True)\n\n    def assemble_as_sql(self, fields, value_rows):\n        \"\"\"\n        Take a sequence of N fields and a sequence of M rows of values, and\n        generate placeholder SQL and parameters for each field and value.\n        Return a pair containing:\n         * a sequence of M rows of N SQL placeholder strings, and\n         * a sequence of M rows of corresponding parameter values.\n\n        Each placeholder string may contain any number of '%s' interpolation\n        strings, and each parameter row will contain exactly as many params\n        as the total number of '%s's in the corresponding placeholder row.\n        \"\"\"\n        if not value_rows:\n            return [], []\n\n        # list of (sql, [params]) tuples for each object to be saved\n        # Shape: [n_objs][n_fields][2]\n        get_placeholders = [getattr(field, \"get_placeholder\", None) for field in fields]\n        rows_of_fields_as_sql = (\n            (\n                self.field_as_sql(field, get_placeholder, value)\n                for field, get_placeholder, value in zip(fields, get_placeholders, row)\n            )\n            for row in value_rows\n        )\n\n        # tuple like ([sqls], [[params]s]) for each object to be saved\n        # Shape: [n_objs][2][n_fields]\n        sql_and_param_pair_rows = (zip(*row) for row in rows_of_fields_as_sql)\n\n        # Extract separate lists for placeholders and params.\n        # Each of these has shape [n_objs][n_fields]\n        placeholder_rows, param_rows = zip(*sql_and_param_pair_rows)\n\n        # Params for each field are still lists, and need to be flattened.\n        param_rows = [[p for ps in row for p in ps] for row in param_rows]\n\n        return placeholder_rows, param_rows\n\n    def as_sql(self):\n        # We don't need quote_name_unless_alias() here, since these are all\n        # going to be column names (so we can avoid the extra overhead).\n        qn = self.connection.ops.quote_name\n        opts = self.query.get_meta()\n        insert_statement = self.connection.ops.insert_statement(\n            on_conflict=self.query.on_conflict,\n        )\n        result = [\"%s %s\" % (insert_statement, qn(opts.db_table))]\n        fields = self.query.fields or [opts.pk]\n        result.append(\"(%s)\" % \", \".join(qn(f.column) for f in fields))\n\n        if self.query.fields:\n            value_rows = [\n                [\n                    self.prepare_value(field, self.pre_save_val(field, obj))\n                    for field in fields\n                ]\n                for obj in self.query.objs\n            ]\n        else:\n            # An empty object.\n            value_rows = [\n                [self.connection.ops.pk_default_value()] for _ in self.query.objs\n            ]\n            fields = [None]\n\n        # Currently the backends just accept values when generating bulk\n        # queries and generate their own placeholders. Doing that isn't\n        # necessary and it should be possible to use placeholders and\n        # expressions in bulk inserts too.\n        can_bulk = (\n            not self.returning_fields and self.connection.features.has_bulk_insert\n        )\n\n        placeholder_rows, param_rows = self.assemble_as_sql(fields, value_rows)\n\n        on_conflict_suffix_sql = self.connection.ops.on_conflict_suffix_sql(\n            fields,\n            self.query.on_conflict,\n            (f.column for f in self.query.update_fields),\n            (f.column for f in self.query.unique_fields),\n        )\n        if (\n            self.returning_fields\n            and self.connection.features.can_return_columns_from_insert\n        ):\n            if self.connection.features.can_return_rows_from_bulk_insert:\n                result.append(\n                    self.connection.ops.bulk_insert_sql(fields, placeholder_rows)\n                )\n                params = param_rows\n            else:\n                result.append(\"VALUES (%s)\" % \", \".join(placeholder_rows[0]))\n                params = [param_rows[0]]\n            if on_conflict_suffix_sql:\n                result.append(on_conflict_suffix_sql)\n            # Skip empty r_sql to allow subclasses to customize behavior for\n            # 3rd party backends. Refs #19096.\n            r_sql, self.returning_params = self.connection.ops.return_insert_columns(\n                self.returning_fields\n            )\n            if r_sql:\n                result.append(r_sql)\n                params += [self.returning_params]\n            return [(\" \".join(result), tuple(chain.from_iterable(params)))]\n\n        if can_bulk:\n            result.append(self.connection.ops.bulk_insert_sql(fields, placeholder_rows))\n            if on_conflict_suffix_sql:\n                result.append(on_conflict_suffix_sql)\n            return [(\" \".join(result), tuple(p for ps in param_rows for p in ps))]\n        else:\n            if on_conflict_suffix_sql:\n                result.append(on_conflict_suffix_sql)\n            return [\n                (\" \".join(result + [\"VALUES (%s)\" % \", \".join(p)]), vals)\n                for p, vals in zip(placeholder_rows, param_rows)\n            ]\n\n    def execute_sql(self, returning_fields=None):\n        assert not (\n            returning_fields\n            and len(self.query.objs) != 1\n            and not self.connection.features.can_return_rows_from_bulk_insert\n        )\n        opts = self.query.get_meta()\n        self.returning_fields = returning_fields\n        cols = []\n        with self.connection.cursor() as cursor:\n            for sql, params in self.as_sql():\n                cursor.execute(sql, params)\n            if not self.returning_fields:\n                return []\n            if (\n                self.connection.features.can_return_rows_from_bulk_insert\n                and len(self.query.objs) > 1\n            ):\n                rows = self.connection.ops.fetch_returned_insert_rows(cursor)\n                cols = [field.get_col(opts.db_table) for field in self.returning_fields]\n            elif self.connection.features.can_return_columns_from_insert:\n                assert len(self.query.objs) == 1\n                rows = [\n                    self.connection.ops.fetch_returned_insert_columns(\n                        cursor,\n                        self.returning_params,\n                    )\n                ]\n                cols = [field.get_col(opts.db_table) for field in self.returning_fields]\n            elif returning_fields and isinstance(\n                returning_field := returning_fields[0], AutoField\n            ):\n                cols = [returning_field.get_col(opts.db_table)]\n                rows = [\n                    (\n                        self.connection.ops.last_insert_id(\n                            cursor,\n                            opts.db_table,\n                            returning_field.column,\n                        ),\n                    )\n                ]\n            else:\n                # Backend doesn't support returning fields and no auto-field\n                # that can be retrieved from `last_insert_id` was specified.\n                return []\n        converters = self.get_converters(cols)\n        if converters:\n            rows = self.apply_converters(rows, converters)\n        return list(rows)\n\n\nclass SQLDeleteCompiler(SQLCompiler):\n    @cached_property\n    def single_alias(self):\n        # Ensure base table is in aliases.\n        self.query.get_initial_alias()\n        return sum(self.query.alias_refcount[t] > 0 for t in self.query.alias_map) == 1\n\n    @classmethod\n    def _expr_refs_base_model(cls, expr, base_model):\n        if isinstance(expr, Query):\n            return expr.model == base_model\n        if not hasattr(expr, \"get_source_expressions\"):\n            return False\n        return any(\n            cls._expr_refs_base_model(source_expr, base_model)\n            for source_expr in expr.get_source_expressions()\n        )\n\n    @cached_property\n    def contains_self_reference_subquery(self):\n        return any(\n            self._expr_refs_base_model(expr, self.query.model)\n            for expr in chain(\n                self.query.annotations.values(), self.query.where.children\n            )\n        )\n\n    def _as_sql(self, query):\n        delete = \"DELETE FROM %s\" % self.quote_name_unless_alias(query.base_table)\n        try:\n            where, params = self.compile(query.where)\n        except FullResultSet:\n            return delete, ()\n        return f\"{delete} WHERE {where}\", tuple(params)\n\n    def as_sql(self):\n        \"\"\"\n        Create the SQL for this query. Return the SQL string and list of\n        parameters.\n        \"\"\"\n        if self.single_alias and (\n            self.connection.features.delete_can_self_reference_subquery\n            or not self.contains_self_reference_subquery\n        ):\n            return self._as_sql(self.query)\n        innerq = self.query.clone()\n        innerq.__class__ = Query\n        innerq.clear_select_clause()\n        pk = self.query.model._meta.pk\n        innerq.select = [pk.get_col(self.query.get_initial_alias())]\n        outerq = Query(self.query.model)\n        if not self.connection.features.update_can_self_select:\n            # Force the materialization of the inner query to allow reference\n            # to the target table on MySQL.\n            sql, params = innerq.get_compiler(connection=self.connection).as_sql()\n            innerq = RawSQL(\"SELECT * FROM (%s) subquery\" % sql, params)\n        outerq.add_filter(\"pk__in\", innerq)\n        return self._as_sql(outerq)\n\n\nclass SQLUpdateCompiler(SQLCompiler):\n    def as_sql(self):\n        \"\"\"\n        Create the SQL for this query. Return the SQL string and list of\n        parameters.\n        \"\"\"\n        self.pre_sql_setup()\n        if not self.query.values:\n            return \"\", ()\n        qn = self.quote_name_unless_alias\n        values, update_params = [], []\n        for field, model, val in self.query.values:\n            if hasattr(val, \"resolve_expression\"):\n                val = val.resolve_expression(\n                    self.query, allow_joins=False, for_save=True\n                )\n                if val.contains_aggregate:\n                    raise FieldError(\n                        \"Aggregate functions are not allowed in this query \"\n                        \"(%s=%r).\" % (field.name, val)\n                    )\n                if val.contains_over_clause:\n                    raise FieldError(\n                        \"Window expressions are not allowed in this query \"\n                        \"(%s=%r).\" % (field.name, val)\n                    )\n                if isinstance(val, ColPairs):\n                    raise FieldError(\n                        \"Composite primary keys expressions are not allowed \"\n                        \"in this query (%s=F('pk')).\" % field.name\n                    )\n            elif hasattr(val, \"prepare_database_save\"):\n                if field.remote_field:\n                    val = val.prepare_database_save(field)\n                else:\n                    raise TypeError(\n                        \"Tried to update field %s with a model instance, %r. \"\n                        \"Use a value compatible with %s.\"\n                        % (field, val, field.__class__.__name__)\n                    )\n            val = field.get_db_prep_save(val, connection=self.connection)\n\n            # Getting the placeholder for the field.\n            if hasattr(field, \"get_placeholder\"):\n                placeholder = field.get_placeholder(val, self, self.connection)\n            else:\n                placeholder = \"%s\"\n            name = field.column\n            if hasattr(val, \"as_sql\"):\n                sql, params = self.compile(val)\n                values.append(\"%s = %s\" % (qn(name), placeholder % sql))\n                update_params.extend(params)\n            elif val is not None:\n                values.append(\"%s = %s\" % (qn(name), placeholder))\n                update_params.append(val)\n            else:\n                values.append(\"%s = NULL\" % qn(name))\n        table = self.query.base_table\n        result = [\n            \"UPDATE %s SET\" % qn(table),\n            \", \".join(values),\n        ]\n        try:\n            where, params = self.compile(self.query.where)\n        except FullResultSet:\n            params = []\n        else:\n            result.append(\"WHERE %s\" % where)\n        return \" \".join(result), tuple(update_params + params)\n\n    def execute_sql(self, result_type):\n        \"\"\"\n        Execute the specified update. Return the number of rows affected by\n        the primary update query. The \"primary update query\" is the first\n        non-empty query that is executed. Row counts for any subsequent,\n        related queries are not available.\n        \"\"\"\n        row_count = super().execute_sql(result_type)\n        is_empty = row_count is None\n        row_count = row_count or 0\n\n        for query in self.query.get_related_updates():\n            # If the result_type is NO_RESULTS then the aux_row_count is None.\n            aux_row_count = query.get_compiler(self.using).execute_sql(result_type)\n            if is_empty and aux_row_count:\n                # Returns the row count for any related updates as the number of\n                # rows updated.\n                row_count = aux_row_count\n                is_empty = False\n        return row_count\n\n    def pre_sql_setup(self):\n        \"\"\"\n        If the update depends on results from other tables, munge the \"where\"\n        conditions to match the format required for (portable) SQL updates.\n\n        If multiple updates are required, pull out the id values to update at\n        this point so that they don't change as a result of the progressive\n        updates.\n        \"\"\"\n        refcounts_before = self.query.alias_refcount.copy()\n        # Ensure base table is in the query\n        self.query.get_initial_alias()\n        count = self.query.count_active_tables()\n        if not self.query.related_updates and count == 1:\n            return\n        query = self.query.chain(klass=Query)\n        query.select_related = False\n        query.clear_ordering(force=True)\n        query.extra = {}\n        query.select = []\n        meta = query.get_meta()\n        fields = [meta.pk.name]\n        related_ids_index = []\n        for related in self.query.related_updates:\n            if all(\n                path.join_field.primary_key for path in meta.get_path_to_parent(related)\n            ):\n                # If a primary key chain exists to the targeted related update,\n                # then the meta.pk value can be used for it.\n                related_ids_index.append((related, 0))\n            else:\n                # This branch will only be reached when updating a field of an\n                # ancestor that is not part of the primary key chain of a MTI\n                # tree.\n                related_ids_index.append((related, len(fields)))\n                fields.append(related._meta.pk.name)\n        query.add_fields(fields)\n        super().pre_sql_setup()\n\n        is_composite_pk = meta.is_composite_pk\n        must_pre_select = (\n            count > 1 and not self.connection.features.update_can_self_select\n        )\n\n        # Now we adjust the current query: reset the where clause and get rid\n        # of all the tables we don't need (since they're in the sub-select).\n        self.query.clear_where()\n        if self.query.related_updates or must_pre_select:\n            # Either we're using the idents in multiple update queries (so\n            # don't want them to change), or the db backend doesn't support\n            # selecting from the updating table (e.g. MySQL).\n            idents = []\n            related_ids = collections.defaultdict(list)\n            for rows in query.get_compiler(self.using).execute_sql(MULTI):\n                pks = [row if is_composite_pk else row[0] for row in rows]\n                idents.extend(pks)\n                for parent, index in related_ids_index:\n                    related_ids[parent].extend(r[index] for r in rows)\n            self.query.add_filter(\"pk__in\", idents)\n            self.query.related_ids = related_ids\n        else:\n            # The fast path. Filters and updates in one query.\n            self.query.add_filter(\"pk__in\", query)\n        self.query.reset_refcounts(refcounts_before)\n\n\nclass SQLAggregateCompiler(SQLCompiler):\n    def as_sql(self):\n        \"\"\"\n        Create the SQL for this query. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_01NtnweEY3rbCtaM4rnPEQD8","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01WdG8w4pRNXjiYoeXjNGKbp","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/logs/agent/solution.md","content":"# Django select_for_update(of) with values_list() and Annotation Expressions - Bug Analysis\n\n## Files Examined\n\n- **django/db/models/sql/compiler.py** — Main entry point; contains `get_select()` method that builds the SELECT clause and `get_select_for_update_of_arguments()` that processes the FOR UPDATE OF clause\n- **django/db/models/expressions.py** — Examined Col and Func classes to understand expression types\n- **django/db/models/functions/text.py** — Contains Concat and other annotation expression classes\n- **tests/select_for_update/models.py** — Test models including Person\n- **tests/select_for_update/tests.py** — Test suite; added reproduction test case\n\n## Dependency Chain\n\n1. **Symptom observed in**: `django/db/models/sql/compiler.py` — `get_select_for_update_of_arguments()` method (line 1405)\n   - When called with a queryset using `select_for_update(of=(\"self\",)).values_list(Concat(...), \"field\")`\n   - Crashes with `AttributeError: 'Concat' object has no attribute 'target'`\n\n2. **Called from**: `django/db/models/sql/compiler.py` — `as_sql()` method (line 884)\n   - Invokes `get_select_for_update_of_arguments()` to generate the FOR UPDATE OF clause\n\n3. **Bug triggered by**: `django/db/models/sql/compiler.py` — `get_select()` method (lines 269-295)\n   - When `self.query.selected is not None` (from `values_list()`), annotation expressions can appear at indexes in the SELECT clause that are incorrectly included in `klass_info[\"select_fields\"]`\n\n4. **Root cause in**: `django/db/models/sql/compiler.py` — `_get_first_selected_col_from_model()` nested function (line 1430-1441)\n   - Iterates through `klass_info[\"select_fields\"]` assuming all items have a `.target` attribute\n   - But when annotations precede model columns in the SELECT list, this breaks\n\n## Root Cause\n\n- **File**: `django/db/models/sql/compiler.py`\n- **Method**: `get_select()` (lines 269-295)\n- **Specific Issue**: Lines 259-268 where `klass_info[\"select_fields\"]` is calculated\n- **Line**: ~262-267\n\n### Detailed Explanation\n\nWhen `values_list()` or `values()` is used with specific fields (including annotations), the queryset sets `self.query.selected` to a dictionary mapping aliases to expressions or column indexes:\n\n```python\n# For: Person.objects.select_for_update(of=(\"self\",)).values_list(\n#          Concat(Value(\"Dr. \"), F(\"name\")), \"born\"\n#      )\nquery.selected = {'concat1': 'concat1', 'born': 0}\nquery.annotation_select = {'concat1': Concat(...)}\n```\n\nThe `get_select()` method builds the SELECT clause from this, but the bug occurs due to a stale assumption about column ordering:\n\n1. **Initial assumption (lines 259-268)**: `klass_info[\"select_fields\"]` is calculated as a range assuming all model columns (`cols`) will occupy a contiguous block of indexes starting at `len(self.query.extra_select)`.\n\n   ```python\n   klass_info = {\n       \"model\": self.query.model,\n       \"select_fields\": list(\n           range(\n               len(self.query.extra_select),\n               len(self.query.extra_select) + len(cols),\n           )\n       ),\n   }\n   ```\n\n2. **Reality with `self.query.selected is not None`** (lines 280-290): The actual order of items in the SELECT clause is determined by iteration order of `self.query.selected.items()`, which in Python 3.7+ is insertion order. This order may NOT correspond to the assumed range because:\n   - Annotations can appear before, between, or after model columns\n   - The dictionary key order doesn't guarantee model columns come first\n\n3. **Concrete Example**:\n   - `query.selected = {'concat1': 'concat1', 'born': 0}` has 'concat1' FIRST\n   - After building the `selected` list:\n     - Index 0: (alias='concat1', expression=Concat(...))  ← NOT a Col, no .target\n     - Index 1: (alias='born', expression=Col(...))         ← Is a Col, has .target\n   - But `klass_info[\"select_fields\"]` still includes [0, 1] based on the range calculation\n   - When `_get_first_selected_col_from_model()` iterates select_fields [0, 1], it tries to access `.target.model` on the Concat at index 0, causing AttributeError\n\n## Proposed Fix\n\n### Change Required\n\nIn `django/db/models/sql/compiler.py`:\n\n1. **Add Col to imports** (line 10):\n```python\nfrom django.db.models.expressions import Col, ColPairs, F, OrderBy, RawSQL, Ref, Value\n```\n\n2. **Recalculate select_fields when self.query.selected is not None** (after line 295):\n```python\n# When self.query.selected is not None (e.g., from values_list()), the\n# order of items in the select list may be different from the default\n# (with annotations appearing before model columns). Recalculate\n# select_fields to only include model columns, not annotations.\nif self.query.selected is not None and klass_info:\n    klass_info[\"select_fields\"] = [\n        select_idx\n        for select_idx, (_, expression) in enumerate(selected)\n        if isinstance(expression, Col)\n        and (\n            expression.target.model == klass_info[\"model\"]\n            or expression.target.model\n            in klass_info[\"model\"]._meta.all_parents\n        )\n    ]\n```\n\n### Full Diff\n\n```diff\n--- a/django/db/models/sql/compiler.py\n+++ b/django/db/models/sql/compiler.py\n@@ -7,1 +7,1 @@\n-from django.db.models.expressions import ColPairs, F, OrderBy, RawSQL, Ref, Value\n+from django.db.models.expressions import Col, ColPairs, F, OrderBy, RawSQL, Ref, Value\n\n@@ -295,0 +296,18 @@\n         select.append((expression, alias))\n+\n+        # When self.query.selected is not None (e.g., from values_list()), the\n+        # order of items in the select list may be different from the default\n+        # (with annotations appearing before model columns). Recalculate\n+        # select_fields to only include model columns, not annotations.\n+        if self.query.selected is not None and klass_info:\n+            klass_info[\"select_fields\"] = [\n+                select_idx\n+                for select_idx, (_, expression) in enumerate(selected)\n+                if isinstance(expression, Col)\n+                and (\n+                    expression.target.model == klass_info[\"model\"]\n+                    or expression.target.model\n+                    in klass_info[\"model\"]._meta.all_parents\n+                )\n+            ]\n```\n\n## Analysis\n\n### How the Bug Manifests\n\nThe bug occurs in a specific sequence:\n\n1. **User writes**:\n   ```python\n   Person.objects.select_for_update(of=(\"self\",)).values_list(\n       Concat(Value(\"Dr. \"), F(\"name\")), \"born\"\n   )\n   ```\n\n2. **Django processes the query**:\n   - `values_list()` sets `query.selected` to explicitly request certain expressions\n   - `select_for_update(of=(\"self\",))` marks that the Person table should be locked\n\n3. **During query compilation** (`setup_query()` at line 74):\n   - `get_select()` is called to build the SELECT clause\n   - `klass_info` is created to track which select indexes belong to the base model\n   - Due to the stale range-based calculation, `klass_info[\"select_fields\"]` includes annotation indexes\n\n4. **During SQL generation** (`as_sql()` at line 884):\n   - `get_select_for_update_of_arguments()` is called to generate the FOR UPDATE OF clause\n   - It calls `_get_first_selected_col_from_model()` which iterates through `klass_info[\"select_fields\"]`\n   - At the first annotation index, it tries to access `.target.model` on a non-Col expression\n   - **AttributeError is raised** because Func expressions (Concat, etc.) don't have a `.target` attribute\n\n### Why This Wasn't Caught Before\n\nPrior to the change in field ordering in `values()`/`values_list()`, the code could rely on an implicit guarantee:\n- Model field columns were ALWAYS placed before annotation expressions in the SELECT clause\n- Therefore, the range-based calculation of `select_fields` was always correct\n\nThe recent change (likely in Django 5.x based on the version context) allows arbitrary field ordering when using `values()`/`values_list()`, breaking this assumption.\n\n### Why the Fix Works\n\nThe fix:\n1. Detects when `self.query.selected is not None` (indicating `values()`/`values_list()` was used)\n2. **Recalculates** `klass_info[\"select_fields\"]` by iterating through the actual `selected` list\n3. **Only includes indexes** of items that are:\n   - `Col` instances (actual model/field columns), AND\n   - From the model itself or its parent models\n4. **Excludes** all other items (annotations, function results, etc.)\n\nThis ensures `get_select_for_update_of_arguments()` only attempts to access `.target.model` on items that actually have a `.target` attribute.\n\n### Testing\n\nCreated test in `tests/select_for_update/tests.py`:\n```python\n@skipUnlessDBFeature(\"has_select_for_update_of\")\ndef test_for_update_of_with_annotation_values_list(self):\n    \"\"\"\n    select_for_update(of=(\"self\",)) should work with values_list()\n    containing annotation expressions.\n    \"\"\"\n    with transaction.atomic():\n        # This should not raise an AttributeError on .target.model\n        values = list(\n            Person.objects.select_for_update(of=(\"self\",)).values_list(\n                Concat(Value(\"Dr. \"), F(\"name\")), \"born\"\n            )\n        )\n    self.assertEqual(len(values), 1)\n    self.assertEqual(values[0][0], \"Dr. Reinhardt\")\n    self.assertEqual(values[0][1], self.city1.id)\n```\n\n### Verification\n\nAll test cases pass:\n1. ✓ `values_list()` with annotations + `select_for_update()` — now works\n2. ✓ `values_list()` without annotations — still works\n3. ✓ `values_list()` with only model columns — still works\n4. ✓ Existing `select_for_update()` tests — still pass\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":125,"cache_read_input_tokens":66221,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":125},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"220e8778-d88b-4486-ab52-1611670ee76a","uuid":"13844d47-4342-4937-add7-89870a47e425"}

Recalculate\n+        # select_fields to only include model columns, not annotations.\n+        if self.query.selected is not None and klass_info:\n+            klass_info[\"select_fields\"] = [\n+                select_idx\n+                for select_idx, (_, expression) in enumerate(selected)\n+                if isinstance(expression, Col)\n+                and (\n+                    expression.target.model == klass_info[\"model\"]\n+                    or expression.target.model\n+                    in klass_info[\"model\"]._meta.all_parents\n+                )\n+            ]\n```\n\n## Analysis\n\n### How the Bug Manifests\n\nThe bug occurs in a specific sequence:\n\n1. **User writes**:\n   ```python\n   Person.objects.select_for_update(of=(\"self\",)).values_list(\n       Concat(Value(\"Dr. \"), F(\"name\")), \"born\"\n   )\n   ```\n\n2. **Django processes the query**:\n   - `values_list()` sets `query.selected` to explicitly request certain expressions\n   - `select_for_update(of=(\"self\",))` marks that the Person table should be locked\n\n3. **During query compilation** (`setup_query()` at line 74):\n   - `get_select()` is called to build the SELECT clause\n   - `klass_info` is created to track which select indexes belong to the base model\n   - Due to the stale range-based calculation, `klass_info[\"select_fields\"]` includes annotation indexes\n\n4. **During SQL generation** (`as_sql()` at line 884):\n   - `get_select_for_update_of_arguments()` is called to generate the FOR UPDATE OF clause\n   - It calls `_get_first_selected_col_from_model()` which iterates through `klass_info[\"select_fields\"]`\n   - At the first annotation index, it tries to access `.target.model` on a non-Col expression\n   - **AttributeError is raised** because Func expressions (Concat, etc.) don't have a `.target` attribute\n\n### Why This Wasn't Caught Before\n\nPrior to the change in field ordering in `values()`/`values_list()`, the code could rely on an implicit guarantee:\n- Model field columns were ALWAYS placed before annotation expressions in the SELECT clause\n- Therefore, the range-based calculation of `select_fields` was always correct\n\nThe recent change (likely in Django 5.x based on the version context) allows arbitrary field ordering when using `values()`/`values_list()`, breaking this assumption.\n\n### Why the Fix Works\n\nThe fix:\n1. Detects when `self.query.selected is not None` (indicating `values()`/`values_list()` was used)\n2. **Recalculates** `klass_info[\"select_fields\"]` by iterating through the actual `selected` list\n3. **Only includes indexes** of items that are:\n   - `Col` instances (actual model/field columns), AND\n   - From the model itself or its parent models\n4. **Excludes** all other items (annotations, function results, etc.)\n\nThis ensures `get_select_for_update_of_arguments()` only attempts to access `.target.model` on items that actually have a `.target` attribute.\n\n### Testing\n\nCreated test in `tests/select_for_update/tests.py`:\n```python\n@skipUnlessDBFeature(\"has_select_for_update_of\")\ndef test_for_update_of_with_annotation_values_list(self):\n    \"\"\"\n    select_for_update(of=(\"self\",)) should work with values_list()\n    containing annotation expressions.\n    \"\"\"\n    with transaction.atomic():\n        # This should not raise an AttributeError on .target.model\n        values = list(\n            Person.objects.select_for_update(of=(\"self\",)).values_list(\n                Concat(Value(\"Dr. \"), F(\"name\")), \"born\"\n            )\n        )\n    self.assertEqual(len(values), 1)\n    self.assertEqual(values[0][0], \"Dr. Reinhardt\")\n    self.assertEqual(values[0][1], self.city1.id)\n```\n\n### Verification\n\nAll test cases pass:\n1. ✓ `values_list()` with annotations + `select_for_update()` — now works\n2. ✓ `values_list()` without annotations — still works\n3. ✓ `values_list()` with only model columns — still works\n4. ✓ Existing `select_for_update()` tests — still pass\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

block of indexes starting at `len(self.query.extra_select)`.\n    49→\n    50→   ```python\n    51→   klass_info = {\n    52→       \"model\": self.query.model,\n    53→       \"select_fields\": }\n    60→   ```\n    61→\n    62→2. **Reality with `self.query.selected is not None`** (lines 280-290): The actual order of items in the SELECT clause is determined by iteration order of `self.query.selected.items()`, which in Python 3.7+ is insertion order. This order may NOT correspond to the assumed range because:\n    63→   - Annotations can appear before, between, or after model columns\n    64→   - The dictionary key order doesn't guarantee model columns come first\n    65→\n    66→3. **Concrete Example**:\n    67→   - `query.selected = {'concat1': 'concat1', 'born': 0}` has 'concat1' FIRST\n    68→   - After building the `selected` list:\n    69→     - Index 0: (alias='concat1', expression=Concat(...))  ← NOT a Col, no .target\n    70→     - Index 1: (alias='born', expression=Col(...))         ← Is a Col, has .target\n    71→   - But `klass_info[\"select_fields\"]` still includes [0, 1] based on the range calculation\n    72→   - When `_get_first_selected_col_from_model()` iterates select_fields [0, 1], it tries to access `.target.model` on the Concat at index 0, causing AttributeError\n    73→\n    74→## Proposed Fix\n    75→\n    76→### Change Required\n    77→\n    78→In `django/db/models/sql/compiler.py`:\n    79→\n    80→1. **Add Col to imports** (line 10):\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware.
